        Idea:
Implement Phase 8 (Inbox surface) of the ArtAgents orchestrator V1 design per docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md section 12 (Phase 8).

LAUNCHER: every `megaplan` call = `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ...`.

DRIVING DISCIPLINE: After every CLI call, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` and `megaplan progress --plan <name>`. If `state == finalized` and `batches_remaining > 0`, you MUST run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --batch N` for each remaining batch sequentially. DO NOT EXIT while state != done.


PHASE 8 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- `runs/<run-id>/inbox/` directory for external completion-signal protocol.
- Files dropped into the inbox by external processes (humans, scripts, other tools) signal that an attested step has completed.
- Inbox files validate into events on the next `next`/`status`/`ack` call.
- Stale or malformed files are ignored (logged but do not crash).
- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, lifecycle status/next handlers.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Inbox files validate into events.
- Stale or malformed files are ignored.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. Define inbox file format. A simple JSON shape:
   ```json
   {
     "step_id": "...",
     "decision": "approve" | "retry" | "abort",
     "evidence": { ... step-specific artifacts ... },
     "submitted_at": "ISO 8601",
     "submitted_by": "<external-system-name>"
   }
   ```

2. New module `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`:
   - `inbox_dir(run_dir) -> Path` — returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`
   - `scan_inbox(run_dir) -> list[InboxEntry]` — reads, validates, returns parsed entries. Malformed files are logged and skipped, not raised.
   - `consume_inbox_entry(run_dir, entry) -> EventRecord` — validates the entry against the current step expectations, writes a hash-chained event, then moves the inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<hash>` (or deletes — pick one and document it).

3. Hook into lifecycle handlers:
   - `artagents next`: scans inbox before computing next step. If the inbox has a valid entry for the current attested step, consume it (record event), then advance the cursor.
   - `artagents status`: scans inbox in read-only mode and surfaces "X inbox entries pending" in output.
   - `artagents ack`: not changed (ack is the explicit verb; inbox is the implicit signal channel).

4. Tests:
   - `tests/test_inbox_scan.py`: drops 3 valid + 1 malformed file, scan returns 3 valid entries and ignores the malformed one without raising.
   - `tests/test_inbox_consume.py`: full flow — attested step waits, drop a valid inbox entry, call `next`, assert event is recorded and cursor advances.
   - `tests/test_inbox_stale.py`: drop an entry referencing a step_id that doesn't match current step, assert it's ignored (logged, not consumed).

5. `runs/<run-id>/AGENT.md` template should mention the inbox surface for human/external operators.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Stay within Phase 8 scope. Do NOT touch Phase 9 golden tests.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies.
- Inbox is opt-in: presence of `inbox/` directory triggers scanning, absence skips it.
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state.

STOP CONDITION: Phase 8 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new inbox tests + status/next surface inbox entries appropriately.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Phase 8 — Inbox Surface

## Overview
Add an opt-in file-based external completion-signal protocol under `runs/<run-id>/inbox/`. External processes (humans, scripts, other tools) drop JSON files into this directory. On the next `next`/`status` call, `inbox.py` scans, validates, and consumes valid entries by recording hash-chained events to `events.jsonl` — advancing the cursor as if an explicit `ack` had been issued. Malformed or stale files are logged and skipped without crashing. The inbox directory triggers scanning only when it exists; its absence is a no-op.

**Key constraints**: additive only, no new dependencies, Phase 8 scope only, existing tests must pass, opt-in by directory presence.

## Phase 1: Core Inbox Module

### Step 1: Create `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`
**Scope:** Medium (~120 lines)

Implement the three core functions:

1. **`inbox_dir(run_dir: Path) -> Path`** — returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`.

2. **`InboxEntry` dataclass** — parsed representation:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class InboxEntry:
       step_id: str
       decision: str  # "approve" | "retry" | "abort"
       evidence: dict[str, Any]
       submitted_at: str  # ISO 8601
       submitted_by: str
       file_path: Path  # original file that was parsed
   ```

3. **`scan_inbox(run_dir: Path) -> list[InboxEntry]`** — reads all `.json` files in `inbox_dir(run_dir)`, validates structure, logs and skips malformed files (no raise). Returns parsed entries.

4. **`consume_inbox_entry(run_dir: Path, entry: InboxEntry, *, plan, events_path: Path, slug: str, projects_root: Path | None = None) -> bool`** — validates the entry against the current step expectations using `peek_current_step`, constructs the appropriate incoming command string (matching the pattern in `_ack_approve`), calls `gate_command` to record the event, then moves the consumed file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<original-filename>-<sha256-trunc8>`. Returns True on success, False if entry doesn't match current step (logged, not consumed).

5. **`consume_pending_inbox(run_dir: Path, *, plan, events_path: Path, slug: str, projects_root: Path | None = None) -> int`** — convenience wrapper: scans inbox, consumes each matching entry, returns count of consumed entries. This is the single call site for lifecycle handlers.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/inbox.py` (new)

### Step 2: Add `make_inbox_consumed_event` to `artagents/core/task/events.py`
**Scope:** Tiny (~10 lines)

Add a new event kind `inbox_consumed` to the events module so inbox consumption is recorded in the hash chain:
```python
def make_inbox_consumed_event(
    plan_step_path: tuple[str, ...],
    *,
    decision: str,
    submitted_by: str,
    submitted_at: str,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
```

**File**: `artagents/core/task/events.py` (append new function near line 330)

### Step 3: Export new symbols from `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`
**Scope:** Tiny (~5 lines)

Add `InboxEntry`, `consume_pending_inbox`, `inbox_dir`, `scan_inbox` to the module exports. Add `make_inbox_consumed_event` to the events exports.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`

## Phase 2: Lifecycle Hooks

### Step 4: Hook `cmd_next` to consume inbox before computing next step
**Scope:** Small (~8 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, inside `cmd_next` (around line 400, after `events = read_events(events_path)` and before `peek = peek_current_step(...)`), add:
```python
# Phase 8: consume pending inbox entries before computing next step
run_dir = proj_root / "runs" / run_id
consumed = consume_pending_inbox(run_dir, plan=plan, events_path=events_path, slug=slug, projects_root=projects_root)
if consumed:
    events = read_events(events_path)  # re-read after consumption
```

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 407)

### Step 5: Hook `cmd_status` to surface pending inbox count
**Scope:** Small (~8 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, inside `cmd_status` (around line 278, after `events = read_events(events_path)`), add:
```python
# Phase 8: surface pending inbox entries
inbox_path = proj_root / "runs" / run_id / "inbox"
if inbox_path.is_dir():
    pending = [p for p in inbox_path.iterdir() if p.suffix == ".json" and p.is_file()]
    if pending:
        print(f"inbox:     {len(pending)} pending entr{'ies' if len(pending) != 1 else 'y'}")
```

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 278)

### Step 6: Update `AGENT.md` template to mention inbox surface
**Scope:** Tiny (~5 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, update `_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE` (line 53) to add a note about the inbox surface under the RECOVERY COMMANDS section:
```
EXTERNAL INBOX
- Drop a JSON file into runs/<run-id>/inbox/ to signal completion without
  using the CLI. See docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md §12 for the file format.
  The inbox is scanned on every `artagents next` and `artagents status` call.
```

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (append to `_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE` after line 68)

## Phase 3: Tests

### Step 7: Test inbox scan (`tests/test_inbox_scan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (~60 lines)

Create test file with:
- **`test_scan_inbox_returns_valid_entries_skips_malformed`**: drops 3 valid JSON files + 1 malformed (invalid JSON), calls `scan_inbox`, asserts 3 entries returned, malformed is skipped without raising.
- **`test_scan_inbox_returns_empty_when_dir_absent`**: no inbox dir, returns empty list.
- **`test_scan_inbox_validates_required_fields`**: drops files missing `step_id`, `decision`, etc.; each is skipped.

**File**: `tests/test_inbox_scan.py` (new)

### Step 8: Test inbox consume (`tests/test_inbox_consume.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (~80 lines)

Create test file with:
- **`test_consume_inbox_entry_advances_cursor`**: sets up an attested plan via `_activate_attested_plan`, drops a valid inbox file referencing the correct `step_id`, calls `consume_pending_inbox`, asserts an `inbox_consumed` + `step_attested` event was written and cursor advanced.
- **`test_consume_inbox_entry_moves_file_to_consumed`**: after consumption, original file is moved to `.consumed/` subdirectory.
- **`test_consume_inbox_approve_decision_works`**: decision=approve produces correct events.

**File**: `tests/test_inbox_consume.py` (new)

### Step 9: Test inbox stale entries (`tests/test_inbox_stale.py`)
**Scope:** Small (~40 lines)

Create test file with:
- **`test_stale_entry_referencing_wrong_step_is_ignored`**: setup plan with step `s1`, drop inbox file referencing `s2` (non-existent step), assert `consume_pending_inbox` returns 0 consumed, file left in inbox (not consumed).
- **`test_stale_entry_after_plan_advanced_is_ignored`**: advance past `s1`, drop inbox file for `s1`, assert it's ignored (stale).

**File**: `tests/test_inbox_stale.py` (new)

### Step 10: Test lifecycle surface (`tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Small (~50 lines)

Create test file with:
- **`test_cmd_next_consumes_inbox_before_peek`**: integration test using the actual `cmd_next`/`cmd_status` CLI functions, asserts inbox entry is consumed and cursor advances.
- **`test_cmd_status_shows_pending_inbox_count`**: drops 2 inbox files, calls `cmd_status`, asserts output contains "inbox: 2 pending entries".

**File**: `tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py` (new)

## Execution Order
1. **Step 1** (inbox.py module) — foundation, no dependencies on other changes.
2. **Step 2** (events.py addition) — required before Step 1 can call `append_event` with the new event kind.
3. **Step 3** (__init__.py exports) — after Steps 1-2.
4. **Step 7** (test_inbox_scan.py) — can run immediately after Step 1, validates the parser in isolation.
5. **Step 4** (hook cmd_next) — wires consumption into the lifecycle.
6. **Step 5** (hook cmd_status) — wires inbox count into status output.
7. **Step 6** (AGENT.md template) — documentation, no code impact.
8. **Steps 8-10** (consume, stale, lifecycle tests) — after all hooks are in place.

## Validation Order
1. Run `pytest tests/test_inbox_scan.py -v` after Step 1+2+3.
2. Run `pytest tests/test_inbox_consume.py tests/test_inbox_stale.py -v` after Step 4.
3. Run `pytest tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py -v` after Step 5.
4. Run `pytest tests/ -v` — full suite must pass, no regressions.

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.

PHASE 8 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- `runs/<run-id>/inbox/` directory for external completion-signal protocol.
- Files dropped into the inbox by external processes (humans, scripts, other tools) signal that an attested step has completed.
- Inbox files validate into events on the next `next`/`status`/`ack` call.
- Stale or malformed files are ignored (logged but do not crash).
- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, lifecycle status/next handlers.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Inbox files validate into events.
- Stale or malformed files are ignored.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. Define inbox file format. A simple JSON shape:
   ```json
   {
     "step_id": "...",
     "decision": "approve" | "retry" | "abort",
     "evidence": { ... step-specific artifacts ... },
     "submitted_at": "ISO 8601",
     "submitted_by": "<external-system-name>"
   }
   ```

2. New module `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`:
   - `inbox_dir(run_dir) -> Path` — returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`
   - `scan_inbox(run_dir) -> list[InboxEntry]` — reads, validates, returns parsed entries. Malformed files are logged and skipped, not raised.
   - `consume_inbox_entry(run_dir, entry) -> EventRecord` — validates the entry against the current step expectations, writes a hash-chained event, then moves the inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<hash>` (or deletes — pick one and document it).

3. Hook into lifecycle handlers:
   - `artagents next`: scans inbox before computing next step. If the inbox has a valid entry for the current attested step, consume it (record event), then advance the cursor.
   - `artagents status`: scans inbox in read-only mode and surfaces "X inbox entries pending" in output.
   - `artagents ack`: not changed (ack is the explicit verb; inbox is the implicit signal channel).

   - `tests/test_inbox_stale.py`: drop an entry referencing a step_id that doesn't match current step, assert it's ignored (logged, not consumed).

5. `runs/<run-id>/AGENT.md` template should mention the inbox surface for human/external operators.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Stay within Phase 8 scope. Do NOT touch Phase 9 golden tests.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies.
- Inbox is opt-in: presence of `inbox/` directory triggers scanning, absence skips it.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Phase 8 — Inbox Surface (Revision 2)

## Overview
Add an opt-in file-based external completion-signal protocol under `runs/<run-id>/inbox/`. External processes (humans, scripts, other tools) drop JSON files into this directory. On the next `next` call, `inbox.py` scans, validates, and consumes valid entries by recording hash-chained events to `events.jsonl` — bypassing the `validate_attested_identity` gate since inbox is by definition an external channel (no terminal operator). Stale entries are drained to `.consumed/.stale/` so they never re-surface. Malformed files are logged and skipped. The `cmd_status` surface reports valid pending count via `scan_inbox`. The inbox directory triggers scanning only when it exists; its absence is a no-op.

**Key constraints**: additive only, no new dependencies, Phase 8 scope only, existing tests must pass, opt-in by directory presence.

**Key design decisions from critique resolution**:
- Inbox consumption writes `step_attested` events directly via a new low-level helper (`_record_inbox_attested` in gate.py) that skips `validate_attested_identity`. The inbox file carries its own identity (`attestor_kind`, `submitted_by`), removing the need for env injection.
- `evidence` in inbox format changed from `dict` to `list[str]` (paths) to match `AttestedArgs.evidence: tuple[str, ...]`.
- Inbox file format now requires `attestor_kind` (`"agent"` | `"actor"`) so the consume path knows whether to record with agent or actor identity.
- `retry` and `abort` decisions are explicitly scoped out for Phase 8: logged as unsupported and skipped (not consumed). Only `approve` is consumable.
- Stale entries (wrong step_id, non-attested cursor) are moved to `.consumed/.stale/` on detection — not left in `inbox/` indefinitely.
- `scan_inbox` returns entries sorted by mtime then filename for stable ordering.
- Consumed filenames are `sha256(<content>)[:16]` (content-addressed, no original-filename issues).
- AGENT.md embeds the full inbox schema inline (not just a doc reference) and documents that users create `inbox/` themselves.

## Phase 1: Low-Level Gate Helper

### Step 1: Add `_record_inbox_attested` to `artagents/core/task/gate.py`
**Scope:** Small (~50 lines)

A new function that records a `step_attested` event for an inbox-driven approve without going through `_dispatch_attested` / `validate_attested_identity`. It mirrors the core of `_dispatch_attested` (lines 904-958) but:

1. Takes pre-parsed attestation info directly instead of parsing a command string.
2. Skips `match_attested_command` and `validate_attested_identity` entirely.
3. Still writes the correct `step_attested` (or `item_attested` for for_each frames) event.
4. Still runs inline produces checks.
5. Returns a `GateDecision` so callers can inspect the result.

Signature:
```python
def _record_inbox_attested(
    *,
    slug: str,
    step: AttestedStep,
    path_str: str,
    path_tuple: tuple[str, ...],
    events_path: Path,
    run_id: str,
    project_root: Path,
    attestor_kind: str,
    attestor_id: str,
    evidence: tuple[str, ...],
    iteration: int | None = None,
    item_id: str | None = None,
) -> GateDecision:
```

Implementation references:
- `_dispatch_attested` at gate.py:904-958 for the event-writing + check pattern.
- `make_step_attested_event` at events.py:151.
- `make_item_attested_event` at events.py:301.
- `_run_inline_checks` at gate.py:1100 (already handles production checks).

**File**: `artagents/core/task/gate.py` (insert after `_dispatch_attested` ending at line 973)

### Step 2: Export `_record_inbox_attested` from gate and `__init__.py`
**Scope:** Tiny (~3 lines)

Add `_record_inbox_attested` to the gate imports in `__init__.py` and to `__all__`.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`

## Phase 2: Core Inbox Module

### Step 3: Create `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`
**Scope:** Medium (~150 lines)

Implement the core inbox module with these components:

1. **InboxEntry dataclass**:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class InboxEntry:
       step_id: str
       decision: str  # "approve" | "retry" | "abort"
       attestor_kind: str  # "agent" | "actor"
       evidence: tuple[str, ...]  # paths, aligned with AttestedArgs.evidence
       submitted_at: str  # ISO 8601
       submitted_by: str
       file_path: Path  # original file that was parsed
   ```

2. **Inbox file JSON format** (documented in module docstring):
   ```json
   {
     "step_id": "...",
     "decision": "approve",
     "attestor_kind": "agent" | "actor",
     "submitted_by": "<external-system-name>",
     "submitted_at": "2026-05-05T09:00:00Z",
     "evidence": ["path/to/artifact", ...]
   }
   ```

3. **`inbox_dir(run_dir: Path) -> Path`** — returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`.

4. **`scan_inbox(run_dir: Path) -> list[InboxEntry]`**:
   - Returns `[]` if `inbox_dir(run_dir)` does not exist (opt-in).
   - Reads all `.json` files, sorted by mtime then filename for stable ordering.
   - Validates required fields (`step_id`, `decision`, `attestor_kind`, `submitted_by`, `submitted_at`).
   - `evidence` defaults to `()` if absent or not a list of strings.
   - `attestor_kind` must be `"agent"` or `"actor"`.
   - `decision` must be `"approve"`, `"retry"`, or `"abort"`.
   - Malformed/invalid files are logged at WARNING level and skipped (no raise).

5. **`consume_inbox_entry(run_dir: Path, entry: InboxEntry, *, plan, events_path: Path, slug: str, projects_root: Path) -> bool`**:
   - Derives `project_root = run_dir.parent.parent` and `run_id = run_dir.name`.
   - Calls `peek_current_step(plan, read_events(events_path), slug, project_root=project_root, run_id=run_id)`.
   - **Guard**: if `peek.exhausted` or `not isinstance(peek.step, AttestedStep)`, log and drain entry to `.consumed/.stale/`, return `False`.
   - If `peek.step.id != entry.step_id`, log and drain entry to `.consumed/.stale/`, return `False` (stale).
   - If `entry.decision != "approve"`, log unsupported decision, drain to `.consumed/.stale/`, return `False`.
   - Compute path_tuple from `peek.path_tuple`.
   - Call `_record_inbox_attested(...)` with the step, attestor info, evidence, iteration, item_id from peek.
   - On success: move inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<sha256-hex[:16]>` (content-addressed).
   - Return `True` on success.

6. **`consume_pending_inbox(run_dir: Path, *, plan, events_path: Path, slug: str, projects_root: Path) -> int`**:
   - Scans inbox, calls `consume_inbox_entry` for each. Returns count of consumed entries.
   - Stops after first consumption (one inbox entry advances the cursor; subsequent entries for the same step would be stale). Actually: consume all valid matching entries to drain the inbox, but per scan_inbox ordering, only the first one for the current step wins; subsequent ones for the same step will be stale after the first consume advances the cursor. This is fine — they get drained to `.stale/`.

7. **`_drain_stale(run_dir: Path, entry: InboxEntry, *, reason: str) -> None`**:
   - Helper to move an inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/.stale/<sha256-hex[:16]>`.
   - Logs the reason at INFO level.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/inbox.py` (new)

## Phase 3: Exports

### Step 4: Export inbox symbols from `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`
**Scope:** Tiny (~5 lines)

Add `InboxEntry`, `consume_pending_inbox`, `inbox_dir`, `scan_inbox` to imports and `__all__`.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/__init__.py`

## Phase 4: Lifecycle Hooks

### Step 5: Hook `cmd_next` to consume inbox before computing next step
**Scope:** Small (~10 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, inside `cmd_next` (after line 407, after `events = read_events(events_path)` and before `peek = peek_current_step(...)`), add:
```python
# Phase 8: consume pending inbox entries before computing next step
run_dir = proj_root / "runs" / run_id
consumed = consume_pending_inbox(run_dir, plan=plan, events_path=events_path, slug=slug, projects_root=projects_root)
if consumed:
    # Re-read events after inbox consumption may have advanced the cursor
    events = read_events(events_path)
```

**Note**: The original plan placed this block *after* `peek = peek_current_step(...)`. This revision places it *before* so that inbox consumption can advance the cursor, and then `peek_current_step` sees the updated cursor. However, `consume_pending_inbox` internally calls `peek_current_step` anyway. The external `peek` call after consumption will reflect the new cursor state.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 407)

### Step 6: Hook `cmd_status` to surface valid pending inbox count
**Scope:** Small (~8 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, inside `cmd_status` (after line 278, after `events = read_events(events_path)`), add:
```python
# Phase 8: surface pending inbox entries (valid only)
inbox_path = proj_root / "runs" / run_id / "inbox"
if inbox_path.is_dir():
    pending = scan_inbox(proj_root / "runs" / run_id)
    if pending:
        print(f"inbox:     {len(pending)} pending entr{'ies' if len(pending) != 1 else 'y'}")
```

Uses `scan_inbox` which returns only valid entries, so stale/malformed files do not inflate the count.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 278)

### Step 7: Update `AGENT.md` template with inbox documentation
**Scope:** Small (~10 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, update `_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE` to add an EXTERNAL INBOX section after the RECOVERY COMMANDS section (i.e., after the `artagents abort` line at line 62, before STOP HOOK):
```
EXTERNAL INBOX
- Create the directory:    mkdir -p runs/<run-id>/inbox/
- Drop a JSON file to signal completion from an external system:
  {
    "step_id": "<step-id>",
    "decision": "approve",
    "attestor_kind": "agent" | "actor",
    "submitted_by": "<external-system-name>",
    "submitted_at": "2026-05-05T09:00:00Z",
    "evidence": ["path/to/artifact", ...]
  }
- The inbox is scanned on every `artagents next` call.
  Valid entries are consumed into the event chain; stale or malformed
  files are drained to inbox/.consumed/.stale/ automatically.
```

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 62, before STOP HOOK)

## Execution Order
1. **Step 1** (`_record_inbox_attested` in gate.py) — foundation for consume path.
2. **Step 2** (export from `__init__.py`) — trivial, after Step 1.
3. **Step 3** (`inbox.py` module) — depends on Step 1 for the call to `_record_inbox_attested`.
4. **Step 4** (export inbox symbols from `__init__.py`) — after Step 3.
5. **Step 8** (`test_inbox_scan.py`) — can run immediately after Step 3+4, validates parser in isolation.
6. **Step 5** (hook `cmd_next`) — wires consumption into the lifecycle.
7. **Step 6** (hook `cmd_status`) — wires inbox count into status output.
8. **Step 7** (AGENT.md template) — documentation, no code impact.
9. **Steps 9-11** (consume, stale, lifecycle tests) — after all hooks are in place.

## Validation Order
1. Run `pytest tests/test_inbox_scan.py -v` after Steps 1-4.
2. Run `pytest tests/test_inbox_consume.py tests/test_inbox_stale.py -v` after Step 5.
3. Run `pytest tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py -v` after Step 6.
4. Run `pytest tests/ -v` — full suite must pass, no regressions.

    "idea": "Implement Phase 8 (Inbox surface) of the ArtAgents orchestrator V1 design per docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md section 12 (Phase 8).\n\nLAUNCHER: every `megaplan` call = `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ...`.\n\nDRIVING DISCIPLINE: After every CLI call, run `megaplan status --plan <name>` and `megaplan progress --plan <name>`. If `state == finalized` and `batches_remaining > 0`, you MUST run `megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --batch N` for each remaining batch sequentially. DO NOT EXIT while state != done.\n\n\nPHASE 8 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):\n\n- `runs/<run-id>/inbox/` directory for external completion-signal protocol.\n- Files dropped into the inbox by external processes (humans, scripts, other tools) signal that an attested step has completed.\n- Inbox files validate into events on the next `next`/`status`/`ack` call.\n- Stale or malformed files are ignored (logged but do not crash).\n- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, lifecycle status/next handlers.\n\nEXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):\n- Inbox files validate into events.\n- Stale or malformed files are ignored.\n\nWHAT TO IMPLEMENT:\n\n1. Define inbox file format. A simple JSON shape:\n   ```json\n   {\n     \"step_id\": \"...\",\n     \"decision\": \"approve\" | \"retry\" | \"abort\",\n     \"evidence\": { ... step-specific artifacts ... },\n     \"submitted_at\": \"ISO 8601\",\n     \"submitted_by\": \"<external-system-name>\"\n   }\n   ```\n\n2. New module `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`:\n   - `inbox_dir(run_dir) -> Path` \u2014 returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`\n   - `scan_inbox(run_dir) -> list[InboxEntry]` \u2014 reads, validates, returns parsed entries. Malformed files are logged and skipped, not raised.\n   - `consume_inbox_entry(run_dir, entry) -> EventRecord` \u2014 validates the entry against the current step expectations, writes a hash-chained event, then moves the inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<hash>` (or deletes \u2014 pick one and document it).\n\n3. Hook into lifecycle handlers:\n   - `artagents next`: scans inbox before computing next step. If the inbox has a valid entry for the current attested step, consume it (record event), then advance the cursor.\n   - `artagents status`: scans inbox in read-only mode and surfaces \"X inbox entries pending\" in output.\n   - `artagents ack`: not changed (ack is the explicit verb; inbox is the implicit signal channel).\n\n4. Tests:\n   - `tests/test_inbox_scan.py`: drops 3 valid + 1 malformed file, scan returns 3 valid entries and ignores the malformed one without raising.\n   - `tests/test_inbox_consume.py`: full flow \u2014 attested step waits, drop a valid inbox entry, call `next`, assert event is recorded and cursor advances.\n   - `tests/test_inbox_stale.py`: drop an entry referencing a step_id that doesn't match current step, assert it's ignored (logged, not consumed).\n\n5. `runs/<run-id>/AGENT.md` template should mention the inbox surface for human/external operators.\n\nCONSTRAINTS:\n- Stay within Phase 8 scope. Do NOT touch Phase 9 golden tests.\n- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.\n- No new dependencies.\n- Inbox is opt-in: presence of `inbox/` directory triggers scanning, absence skips it.\n- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state.\n\nSTOP CONDITION: Phase 8 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new inbox tests + status/next surface inbox entries appropriately.",

(4) Stale entries are drained to `.consumed/.stale/` immediately on detection, never left in inbox to inflate `cmd_status` counts. (5) `scan_inbox` sorts by mtime then filename for reproducibility. (6) `cmd_status` calls `scan_inbox` (only valid entries counted) rather than raw `iterdir`. (7) Consumed filenames use content-addressing (`sha256[:16]`) for safety. (8) AGENT.md template embeds schema inline with `mkdir` instructions for opt-in. (9) Added `isinstance(peek.step, AttestedStep)` guard on consume path. (10) New test for inbox consume without `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR` env set. (11) `scan_inbox` explicitly returns `[]` on missing directory.       "id": "scope",
      "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Inbox bypass of validate_attested_identity changes the trust model. The ack path enforces ARTAGENTS_ACTOR equality + self-ack rejection \u2014 a defense-in-depth check that the operator at the terminal really is the named actor. The inbox path completely removes this. Anyone with FS write access to runs/<run-id>/inbox/ can attest as anyone, with no signature or shared secret. In V1's single-host file-based model (per SD-029), this is defensible \u2014 FS permissions ARE the auth \u2014 but the plan never explicitly says so. The trust shift should be documented in the inbox.py module docstring or AGENT.md so operators understand inbox/ permissions are load-bearing.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

PHASE 8 SCOPE (from docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md):

- `runs/<run-id>/inbox/` directory for external completion-signal protocol.
- Files dropped into the inbox by external processes (humans, scripts, other tools) signal that an attested step has completed.
- Inbox files validate into events on the next `next`/`status`/`ack` call.
- Stale or malformed files are ignored (logged but do not crash).
- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, lifecycle status/next handlers.

EXIT CRITERIA (from design doc):
- Inbox files validate into events.
- Stale or malformed files are ignored.

WHAT TO IMPLEMENT:

1. Define inbox file format. A simple JSON shape:
   ```json
   {
     "step_id": "...",
     "decision": "approve" | "retry" | "abort",
     "evidence": { ... step-specific artifacts ... },
     "submitted_at": "ISO 8601",
     "submitted_by": "<external-system-name>"
   }
   ```

2. New module `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`:
   - `inbox_dir(run_dir) -> Path` — returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`
   - `scan_inbox(run_dir) -> list[InboxEntry]` — reads, validates, returns parsed entries. Malformed files are logged and skipped, not raised.
   - `consume_inbox_entry(run_dir, entry) -> EventRecord` — validates the entry against the current step expectations, writes a hash-chained event, then moves the inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<hash>` (or deletes — pick one and document it).

3. Hook into lifecycle handlers:
   - `artagents next`: scans inbox before computing next step. If the inbox has a valid entry for the current attested step, consume it (record event), then advance the cursor.
   - `artagents status`: scans inbox in read-only mode and surfaces "X inbox entries pending" in output.
   - `artagents ack`: not changed (ack is the explicit verb; inbox is the implicit signal channel).

4. Tests:
   - `tests/test_inbox_scan.py`: drops 3 valid + 1 malformed file, scan returns 3 valid entries and ignores the malformed one without raising.
   - `tests/test_inbox_consume.py`: full flow — attested step waits, drop a valid inbox entry, call `next`, assert event is recorded and cursor advances.
   - `tests/test_inbox_stale.py`: drop an entry referencing a step_id that doesn't match current step, assert it's ignored (logged, not consumed).

5. `runs/<run-id>/AGENT.md` template should mention the inbox surface for human/external operators.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Stay within Phase 8 scope. Do NOT touch Phase 9 golden tests.
- Additive only. Existing tests must continue to pass.
- No new dependencies.
- Inbox is opt-in: presence of `inbox/` directory triggers scanning, absence skips it.
- Honor existing patterns: hash-chained events, gate above dispatch, file-based state.

STOP CONDITION: Phase 8 done when `pytest tests/` passes with new inbox tests + status/next surface inbox entries appropriately.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Phase 8 — Inbox Surface (Revision 3)

## Overview

Add an opt-in file-based external completion-signal protocol under `runs/<run-id>/inbox/`. External processes (humans, scripts, other tools) drop JSON files into this directory. On the next `next` call, `inbox.py` scans, validates, and consumes exactly one valid matching entry per call by recording a hash-chained `step_attested` event to `events.jsonl` — bypassing `validate_attested_identity` since inbox is by definition an external channel (no terminal operator identity check). Stale entries (wrong step_id, non-AttestedStep cursor) are drained to `.consumed/.stale/` on detection. Malformed files are logged and skipped at scan time. The `cmd_status` surface reports valid pending count via `count_pending_inbox`, which filters `scan_inbox` results against the current cursor step_id so stale entries never inflate the count. The inbox directory triggers scanning only when it exists; its absence is a no-op.

**Trust model**: In V1's single-host file-based architecture, filesystem permissions on `runs/<run-id>/inbox/` are the auth boundary. Anyone with write access can attest as anyone. This is intentional for Phase 8 — the inbox is an external channel by design, not a terminal-identity-checked channel like `ack`.

**Key design decisions from Revision 2 carried forward**:
- Inbox consumption writes `step_attested` events directly via `record_inbox_attested` in gate.py that skips `validate_attested_identity`. The inbox file carries its own identity (`attestor_kind`, `submitted_by`).
- `evidence` in inbox format is `list[str]` (paths) to match `AttestedArgs.evidence: tuple[str, ...]`. This is a deliberate deviation from the brief's dict shape, required for gate compatibility.
- Inbox file format requires `attestor_kind` (`"agent"` | `"actor"`).
- `retry` and `abort` decisions are scoped out for Phase 8: logged and drained to stale, not consumed. Only `approve` is consumable.
- Stale entries drained to `.consumed/.stale/` on detection.
- `scan_inbox` returns entries sorted by mtime then filename.
- Consumed filenames are content-addressed: `sha256(<content>)[:16]`.
- AGENT.md embeds the full inbox schema inline.

**Key changes in Revision 3**:
1. Renamed `_record_inbox_attested` → `record_inbox_attested` (public name per convention).
2. Returns `bool` instead of `GateDecision` (caller only needs success/failure).
3. Explicitly documents that `record_inbox_attested` is success-only — no `iterate_feedback` handling since inbox `decision` is always `"approve"`.
4. `consume_pending_inbox` consumes exactly one entry per call (not all-matching). After one consumption the cursor advances; subsequent entries for the same step are stale.
5. New `count_pending_inbox(run_dir, *, plan, events, slug, project_root, run_id) -> int` helper that filters `scan_inbox` by current cursor step_id, so `cmd_status` never counts stale entries.
6. `cmd_status` inbox print moved after produces line (before "recent events:").
7. AGENT.md template uses escaped `{{` / `}}` for all JSON braces to prevent `.format()` KeyError.
8. `docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md` Phase 8 section updated with next-only consumption note and evidence format change.
9. Inbox module docstring documents trust model (FS permissions = auth).
10. AGENT.md notes evidence is `list[str]` of paths (deliberate deviation from brief).
11. Explicit documentation that inbox attestation is restricted to cursor's current item (no out-of-order for_each).

**Key constraints**: additive only, no new dependencies, Phase 8 scope only, existing tests must pass, opt-in by directory presence.

## Phase 1: Gate Helper

### Step 1: Add `record_inbox_attested` to `artagents/core/task/gate.py`
**Scope:** Small (~50 lines)

Insert a new public function after `_dispatch_attested` (ending at gate.py:973). It writes a `step_attested` (or `item_attested`) event without going through `validate_attested_identity`. Signature:

```python
def record_inbox_attested(
    *,
    slug: str,
    step: AttestedStep,
    path_str: str,
    path_tuple: tuple[str, ...],
    events_path: Path,
    run_id: str,
    project_root: Path,
    attestor_kind: str,
    attestor_id: str,
    evidence: tuple[str, ...],
    iteration: int | None = None,
    item_id: str | None = None,
) -> bool:
```

Implementation:
1. If `item_id is not None`, call `make_item_attested_event(path_tuple, item_id, attestor_kind=attestor_kind, attestor_id=attestor_id, evidence=evidence)` and `append_event`.
2. Else call `make_step_attested_event(path_str, attestor_kind, attestor_id, evidence)` and `append_event`.
3. dátummal `GateDecision` for inline produces checks: build it same as `_dispatch_attested` at gate.py:945-958.
4. If `step.produces`, call `_run_inline_checks(decision, step.produces)`.
5. **Do NOT handle `iterate_feedback`.** The inbox `decision` is always `"approve"` (success path), so no `iteration_failed` event should be written. Document in function docstring: "This helper is success-only; inbox entries always represent approved attestations. iterate_feedback is not supported — if iteration is in progress, the cursor advances normally."
6. Check `_run_inline_checks` return: if False (produces check failed), return `False`.
7. Return `True` on success.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/gate.py` (insert after line 973)

## Phase 2: Core Inbox Module

### Step 2: Create `artagents/core/task/inbox.py`
**Scope:** Medium (~170 lines)

Implement the inbox module with these components:

**Module docstring** must include trust-model note:
```
In V1's single-host file-based model, filesystem permissions on
runs/<run-id>/inbox/ are the auth boundary. Anyone with write access
can attest as anyone. The inbox bypasses validate_attested_identity
by design — it is an external channel, not a terminal-identity-checked
channel like `ack`.
```

1. **`InboxEntry` dataclass**:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class InboxEntry:
       step_id: str
       decision: str  # "approve" | "retry" | "abort"
       attestor_kind: str  # "agent" | "actor"
       evidence: tuple[str, ...]
       submitted_at: str
       submitted_by: str
       file_path: Path
   ```

2. **`inbox_dir(run_dir: Path) -> Path`** — returns `<run_dir>/inbox/`.

3. **`scan_inbox(run_dir: Path) -> list[InboxEntry]`**:
   - Returns `[]` if `inbox_dir(run_dir)` does not exist.
   - Reads all `.json` files, sorted by `(mtime, filename)`.
   - Validates required fields: `step_id` (str), `decision` (str, one of approve/retry/abort), `attestor_kind` (str, one of agent/actor), `submitted_by` (str), `submitted_at` (str).
   - `evidence` defaults to `()` if absent or not a list of strings.
   - Malformed/invalid files are logged at `WARNING` level and skipped (no raise).
   - Checks `decision` and `attestor_kind` against allowed values; violations logged and skipped.

4. **`count_pending_inbox(run_dir: Path, *, plan: TaskPlan, events: list[dict], slug: str, project_root: Path, run_id: str) -> int`**:
   - Calls `scan_inbox(run_dir)`.
   - If no entries or `inbox_dir` absent, return `0`.
   - Calls `peek_current_step(plan, events, slug, project_root=project_root, run_id=run_id)`.
   - If `peek.exhausted` or `peek.step is None`, return `0`.
   - If `not isinstance(peek.step, AttestedStep)`, return `0`.
   - Count entries where `entry.step_id == peek.step.id`.
   - This ensures stale entries (wrong step_id) never inflate the `cmd_status` count.

5. **`consume_inbox_entry(run_dir: Path, entry: InboxEntry, *, plan: TaskPlan, events_path: Path, slug: str, projects_root: Path) -> bool`**:
   - Derive `project_root = run_dir.parent.parent`, `run_id = run_dir.name`.
   - Read `events = read_events(events_path)`.
   - Call `peek = peek_current_step(plan, events, slug, project_root=project_root, run_id=run_id)`.
   - **Guard 1**: If `peek.exhausted` or `peek.step is None`, log and `_drain_stale`, return `False`.
   - **Guard 2**: If `not isinstance(peek.step, AttestedStep)`, log and `_drain_stale`, return `False`.
   - **Guard 3**: If `peek.step.id != entry.step_id`, log and `_drain_stale`, return `False` (stale).
   - **Guard 4**: If `entry.decision != "approve"`, log unsupported decision, `_drain_stale`, return `False`.
   - Compute `path_str = STEP_PATH_SEP.join(peek.path_tuple)`.
   - Call `record_inbox_attested(slug=slug, step=peek.step, path_str=path_str, path_tuple=peek.path_tuple, events_path=events_path, run_id=run_id, project_root=project_root, attestor_kind=entry.attestor_kind, attestor_id=entry.submitted_by, evidence=entry.evidence, iteration=peek.iteration, item_id=peek.item_id)`.
   - On success: compute `sha256(json.dumps(<entry-data>))[:16]`, move inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/<hash>`.
   - Return `True` on success, `False` otherwise.

6. **`consume_pending_inbox(run_dir: Path, *, plan: TaskPlan, events_path: Path, slug: str, projects_root: Path) -> int`**:
   - Calls `scan_inbox(run_dir)`.
   - Iterates entries in order; calls `consume_inbox_entry` for each.
   - **Returns immediately after first successful consumption** (returns 1). Only one entry per `next` call — after consumption the cursor advances, making subsequent same-step entries stale.
   - Returns `0` if no entry consumed.

7. **`_drain_stale(run_dir: Path, entry: InboxEntry, *, reason: str) -> None`**:
   - Helper to move an inbox file to `<run_dir>/inbox/.consumed/.stale/<sha256-hex[:16]>`.
   - Logs the reason at `INFO` level.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/inbox.py` (new)

## Phase 4: Lifecycle Hooks

### Step 4: Hook `cmd_next` to consume inbox before computing next step
**Scope:** Small (~12 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, inside `cmd_next`, insert after `events = read_events(events_path)` (line 407) and **before** `peek = peek_current_step(...)` (line 408):

```python
# Phase 8: consume pending inbox entries before computing next step
run_dir = proj_root / "runs" / run_id
consumed = consume_pending_inbox(
    run_dir, plan=plan, events_path=events_path, slug=slug, projects_root=projects_root
)
if consumed:
    events = read_events(events_path)  # re-read after cursor advance
```

This ensures inbox consumption advances the cursor before `peek_current_step` derives the updated state for display.

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 407, before line 408)

### Step 5: Hook `cmd_status` to surface valid pending inbox count
**Scope:** Small (~8 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, inside `cmd_status`, insert **after line 300** (after the produces print, before `print("recent events:")` at line 302):

```python
# Phase 8: surface valid pending inbox entries (filtered by current step)
run_dir = proj_root / "runs" / run_id
pending = count_pending_inbox(
    run_dir,
    plan=plan,
    events=events,
    slug=slug,
    project_root=proj_root,
    run_id=run_id,
)
if pending:
    print(f"inbox:     {pending} pending entr{'ies' if pending != 1 else 'y'}")
```

Uses `count_pending_inbox` which filters `scan_inbox` results by current cursor step_id, so stale entries never inflate the count. Output lands after the produces line and before "recent events:".

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 300)

## Phase 5: Documentation

### Step 6: Update `AGENT.md` template with inbox documentation
**Scope:** Small (~15 lines)

In `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py`, update `_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE` (lines 53-69) to add an EXTERNAL INBOX section after the `artagents abort` line (line 62), before STOP HOOK. **All JSON braces MUST be escaped as `{{` and `}}`** to prevent `str.format()` KeyError:

```
EXTERNAL INBOX
- Create the directory:    mkdir -p runs/<run-id>/inbox/
- Drop a JSON file to signal completion from an external system:
  {{
    "step_id": "<step-id>",
    "decision": "approve",
    "attestor_kind": "agent" | "actor",
    "submitted_by": "<external-system-name>",
    "submitted_at": "2026-05-05T09:00:00Z",
    "evidence": ["path/to/artifact", ...]
  }}
- `evidence` is a list of file-path strings (not a dict). This is a deliberate
  deviation from `docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md`'s dict shape; the list form is
  required for gate compatibility with `AttestedArgs.evidence`.
- The inbox is scanned on every `artagents next` call.
  Valid entries are consumed into the event chain; stale or malformed
  files are drained to inbox/.consumed/.stale/ automatically.
- TRUST MODEL: Filesystem permissions on runs/<run-id>/inbox/ are the only
  auth boundary in V1. Anyone with write access can attest as anyone.
  Inbox attestations bypass the `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR` identity check that `ack` enforces.
```

**File**: `artagents/core/task/lifecycle.py` (insert after line 62, before STOP HOOK at line 64)

### Step 7: Update `docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md` Phase 8 section
**Scope:** Tiny (~5 lines)

In `docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md`, update the Phase 8 section (lines 138-144) to reflect implementation decisions:

```markdown
Phase 8 - Inbox surface

- Scope: `runs/<run-id>/inbox/` completion-signal protocol.
- Consumption: inbox entries are consumed on `next` only (not `status` or `ack`).
  `status` is read-only and surfaces valid pending count; `ack` is the explicit
  terminal verb and is unchanged.
- Evidence format: `evidence` is `list[str]` of file paths (deviation from brief's
  dict shape, required for gate compatibility with `AttestedArgs.evidence`).
- Files touched: `artagents/core/task/`, lifecycle status/next handlers.
- Classification: additive.
- Exit criteria: inbox files validate into events; stale or malformed files are ignored.
- Test strategy: inbox parser tests and external-attestation golden run.
```

**File**: `docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md` (replace lines 138-144)

## Phase 6: Tests

### Step 8: Test inbox scan (`tests/test_inbox_scan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (~65 lines)

Create `tests/test_inbox_scan.py`:

- **`test_scan_inbox_returns_valid_entries_skips_malformed(tmp_projects_root)`**: drops 3 valid JSON files + 1 malformed (not valid JSON), calls `scan_inbox`, asserts 3 entries returned, malformed skipped without raise.
- **`test_scan_inbox_returns_empty_when_dir_absent(tmp_projects_root)`**: no inbox dir, `scan_inbox` returns `[]`.
- **`test_scan_inbox_validates_missing_required_fields(tmp_projects_root)`**: drops files missing `step_id`, `attestor_kind`, etc.; each is skipped.
- **`test_scan_inbox_sorts_by_mtime_then_filename(tmp_projects_root)`**: drops files with controlled mtimes, asserts stable sorted order.

### Step 9: Test inbox consume (`tests/test_inbox_consume.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (~100 lines)

Create `tests/test_inbox_consume.py`:

- **`test_consume_inbox_approve_writes_step_attested(tmp_projects_root)`**: sets up attested plan, drops valid inbox file with `decision: "approve"`, calls `consume_pending_inbox`, asserts `step_attested` event written with correct `attestor_kind` and `attestor_id`.
- **`test_consume_inbox_entry_moves_file_to_consumed(tmp_projects_root)`**: after consumption, original file moved to `.consumed/<hash>`.
- **`test_consume_inbox_works_without_artagents_actor_env(tmp_projects_root, monkeypatch)`**: removes `ARTAGENTS_ACTOR` from env, verifies consumption still succeeds (key regression for identity-bypass).
- **`test_consume_inbox_one_per_next_call(tmp_projects_root)`**: drops 2 valid entries for same step, calls `consume_pending_inbox`, asserts only 1 consumed, returns 1, second remains (becomes stale on next scan).
- **`test_consume_inbox_rejects_nested_cursor(tmp_projects_root)`**: cursor on a CodeStep, inbox entry drained to `.stale/`, not consumed, returns 0.
- **`test_consume_inbox_rejects_retry_decision(tmp_projects_root)`**: `decision: "retry"` drained to `.stale/`, not consumed.

### Step 10: Test inbox stale entries (`tests/test_inbox_stale.py`)
**Scope:** Small (~55 lines)

Create `tests/test_inbox_stale.py`:

- **`test_stale_entry_wrong_step_id_is_drained(tmp_projects_root)`**: setup plan with step `s1`, drop inbox file for `s2`, `consume_pending_inbox` returns 0, file moved to `.consumed/.stale/`.
- **`test_stale_entry_after_cursor_advances_still_in_inbox(tmp_projects_root)`**: consume `s1`, thenawn drop another file for `s1` — it's sitting in inbox until next `next`/`consume_pending_inbox`. That's fine.
- **`test_count_pending_inbox_filters_by_current_step(tmp_projects_root)`**: drop 1 entry for current step + 1 stale (different step_id), `count_pending_inbox` returns 1, not 2.

### Step 11: Test lifecycle surface (`tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Small (~65 lines)

Create `tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py`:

- **`test_cmd_next_consumes_inbox_before_peek(tmp_projects_root, capsys)`**: integration test, drops valid inbox file, calls `cmd_next`, asserts cursor advances and output reflects next step.
- **`test_cmd_status_shows_pending_inbox_count(tmp_projects_root, capsys)`**: drops 2 valid inbox files for current step, calls `cmd_status`, output contains "inbox: 2 pending entries".
- **`test_cmd_status_does_not_count_stale_entries(tmp_projects_root, capsys)`**: drops 1 valid (matching current step) + 1 stale (different step_id), calls `cmd_status`, output shows only 1 pending. Uses `count_pending_inbox` which filters by cursor step_id.
- **`test_cmd_status_no_inbox_dir_no_output(tmp_projects_root, capsys)`**: no inbox dir, `cmd_status` output does not mention inbox.

## Execution Order

1. **Step 1** (`record_inbox_attested` in gate.py) — foundation.
2. **Step 2** (`inbox.py` module) — depends on Step 1.
3. **Step 3** (exports) — after Steps 1-2.
4. **Step 8** (`test_inbox_scan.py`) — can run immediately after Step 3.
5. **Step 4** (hook `cmd_next`) — wires consumption.
6. **Step 5** (hook `cmd_status`) — wires count.
7. **Steps 9-10** (consume + stale tests) — after Step 4.
8. **Step 11** (lifecycle tests) — after Steps 4-5.
9. **Step 6** (AGENT.md template) — doc only, any time.
10. **Step 7** (docs update) — doc only, any time.

## Validation Order

1. `pytest tests/test_inbox_scan.py -v` after Steps 1-3.
2. `pytest tests/test_inbox_consume.py tests/test_inbox_stale.py -v` after Step 4.
3. `pytest tests/test_inbox_lifecycle.py -v` after Step 5.
4. `pytest tests/ -v` — full suite must pass, no regressions.

        "resolution": "Revision 2 addresses all 23 open significant flags.     "evidence": "Revision 3 addresses all 11 open significant flags from the gate review. (2) Explicitly documents record_inbox_attested as success-only (no iterate_feedback) since inbox decision is always 'approve'. (3) consume_pending_inbox now consumes exactly one entry per call, returning immediately after first success \u2014 cursor advances, subsequent same-step entries become stale. (4) New count_pending_inbox helper filters scan_inbox by current cursor step_id so cmd_status never counts stale entries.         Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],

        }
        ```
